Algebra 1 — Unit 2 Bank: By I-Can / Parallel View

Same canonical items as the teaching-order Bank, regrouped for comparison.

Teaching Order View

I can construct rate-of-change statements from tables, graphs, and situations.

context_rate

Example 4

U2-S21-NOTESEXAMPLE-04 · Notes Example · family context_rate · Context · DOK 2

The temperature drops 15 degrees over 5 hours. What is the constant rate in degrees per hour?

Answer / solution

The constant rate is $-3$ degrees per hour.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can construct rate-of-change statements from tables, graphs, and situations.
Representation: Context
Evidence/job: Interpret a contextual rate with units.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit PROC-DIRECT-EXECUTE-01

rate_from_table

Example 2

U2-S21-NOTESEXAMPLE-02 · Notes Example · family rate_from_table · Table · DOK 2

Use the table to determine the constant rate of change in $y$ per 1 unit of $x$.

$x$$y$
$0$$8$
$2$$4$
$4$$0$
$6$$-4$
Answer / solution

The rate of change is $-2$ because $y$ changes by $-4$ when $x$ changes by 2.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can construct rate-of-change statements from tables, graphs, and situations.
Representation: Table
Evidence/job: Read constant rate from equal input intervals.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01

wtc_rate_context

WTC

U2-S21-WTC-01 · WTC · family wtc_rate_context · Graph + Context · DOK 2

A student is tracking distance from a starting point during a steady walk. Study the graph before using any formal slope formula.

(a) Describe how the distance changes each minute. (b) Use two visible points to estimate how many blocks are added for each additional minute. (c) Predict the distance at 5 minutes and explain what feature of the graph makes that prediction reasonable.

Answer / solution

The distance increases by 3 blocks each minute. The graph has a constant rise/run of 3 blocks per minute. At 5 minutes the model gives 16 blocks; the straight-line pattern supports the prediction.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can construct rate-of-change statements from tables, graphs, and situations.
Representation: Graph + Context
Evidence/job: Preview constant rate as a measurable feature across a shared graph.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit WTC-FRQ-PREVIEW-01

Question 1

U2-S21-WARMUP3-01 · Warm-Up 3 · family slope_from_graph · Graph · DOK 2

Determine the slope of the line shown. Use two grid points to support your calculation.

Answer / solution

The slope is $\frac{1}{2}$.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can apply slope calculations to interpret constant change between quantities.
Representation: Graph
Evidence/job: Calculate and interpret slope from an actual graph.

slope_from_points

Example 1

U2-S21-NOTESEXAMPLE-01 · Notes Example · family slope_from_points · Equation · DOK 1-2

Answer / solution

$m=-\dfrac{8}{4}=-2$.

slope_sign_special

Example 5

U2-S21-NOTESEXAMPLE-05 · Notes Example · family slope_sign_special · Graph / Context · DOK 1-2

Classify the slope of a line that falls from left to right.

Answer / solution

The slope is negative because $y$ decreases as $x$ increases.

I can determine whether a slope value is reasonable for a given context.

reasonableness_error

Example 6

U2-S21-NOTESEXAMPLE-06 · Notes Example · family reasonableness_error · Context / Equation · DOK 2-3

Two points are $(1,4)$ and $(5,12)$. Sam uses $\frac{12-4}{1-5}=-2$. Identify and repair the subtraction-order error.

Answer / solution

The subtraction order is inconsistent. Using the same point order gives $\frac{12-4}{5-1}=2$.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can determine whether a slope value is reasonable for a given context.
Representation: Context / Equation
Evidence/job: Evaluate slope reasonableness and diagnose a misconception.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit ERROR-CRITIQUE-CLAIM-01

YTI 6

U2-S21-NOTESYTI-06 · Notes YTI · family reasonableness_error · Context / Equation · DOK 2-3

A water level drops 12 cm in 4 minutes. Mia reports a slope of $+3$ cm/min. Decide whether the reported slope is reasonable and correct it if needed.

Answer / solution

The sign is not reasonable for a decreasing quantity. The slope is $-3$ cm/min.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can determine whether a slope value is reasonable for a given context.
Representation: Context / Equation
Evidence/job: Evaluate slope reasonableness and diagnose a misconception.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit ERROR-CRITIQUE-CLAIM-01

Question 16

U2-S21-PRACTICESET1-16 · Practice Set 1 · family reasonableness_error · Context / Equation · DOK 2-3

A runner covers 6 miles in 0.5 hour. Jordan says the slope is 12 hours per mile. Correct the unit interpretation.

Answer / solution

No. The rate for distance versus time is 12 miles per hour; the units were reversed.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can determine whether a slope value is reasonable for a given context.
Representation: Context / Equation
Evidence/job: Evaluate slope reasonableness and diagnose a misconception.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit ERROR-CRITIQUE-CLAIM-01

Question 17

U2-S21-PRACTICESET1-17 · Practice Set 1 · family reasonableness_error · Context / Equation · DOK 2-3

Two points are $(1,1)$ and $(4,16)$. A student computes $\frac{16-1}{1-4}$ and reports the slope as $-5$. Identify and repair the subtraction-order error.

Answer / solution

The numerator and denominator use opposite point orders. Using the same order gives $\frac{16-1}{4-1}=5$.

Representation: Context / Equation
Evidence/job: Evaluate slope reasonableness and diagnose a misconception.

Question 18

U2-S21-PRACTICESET1-18 · Practice Set 1 · family reasonableness_error · Context / Equation · DOK 2-3

A horizontal line through $y=-4$ is said to have undefined slope. Decide whether the stated slope type is correct.

Answer / solution

No. A horizontal line has slope 0; a vertical line has undefined slope.

The rate of change is $8/2=4$ units of $y$ per 1 unit of $x$.

I can apply an input-output rule to generate missing values and ordered pairs.

review_evidence

Question 1

U2-S21-EXITTICKETB-01 · Exit Ticket B · family review_evidence · Table + Equation · DOK 1-2

The rule is $y=3x-3$. Complete the input-output values and name one ordered pair generated by the rule.

$x$$y$
$-2$$-9$
$0$$-3$
$3$$6$
Answer / solution

Each row follows the rule; for example, $(0,-3)$ is an ordered pair.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can apply an input-output rule to generate missing values and ordered pairs.
Representation: Table + Equation
Evidence/job: Review evidence pulse on the exact prior I-can routed for this section.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit REPTRANS-GRAPH-TABLE-EQUATION-01

mg03_classify

V5 Question 3

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-35 · Secure Summative · family mg03_classify · Table Comparison · DOK 2-3

One table has outputs 6, 10, 14, 18 for inputs 0, 1, 2, 3. Another has outputs 6, 10, 16, 24 for the same inputs. Identify which table represents constant rate of change, write an equation for that relationship, and explain your evidence.

Answer / solution

The first table is linear because each output increases by 4. Its equation is $y=4x+6$. The second table has changing first differences.

V6 Question 4

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-44 · Secure Summative · family mg03_verify_representation · Equation + Table / Error Analysis · DOK 2-3

A student says the table represents $y=-2x+9$. Check the claim. If any entry is inconsistent, identify it and give the corrected output. Then state the relationship's constant rate and starting value.

$x$$y$
$0$$9$
$2$$5$
$4$$2$
$6$$-3$
Answer / solution

The row with $x=4$ is inconsistent. The equation gives $y=-2(4)+9=1$, so that row should be $(4,1)$. The constant rate is $-2$ and the starting value is 9.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: Recognize constant rate of change and represent linear relationships with tables, graphs, equations, slope, and starting value.
Representation: Equation + Table / Error Analysis
Evidence/job: Secure independent alternate-form evidence for U2-MG03.
Mastery Goal: U2-MG03
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit ERROR-CRITIQUE-CLAIM-01

V5 Question 6

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-38 · Secure Summative · family mg03_context · Context + Equation + Table · DOK 2-3

A water tank contains 92 liters and loses 6 liters each minute. Represent the relationship with an equation and a table for minutes 0, 5, and 10. Explain what the negative slope means.

Answer / solution

$V=92-6t$. Values: $(0,92)$, $(5,62)$, $(10,32)$. The slope $-6$ means the volume decreases by 6 liters each minute.

V6 Question 3

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-43 · Secure Summative · family mg03_points_model · Ordered Pairs -> Equation · DOK 2-3

A linear relationship passes through $(2,11)$ and $(6,23)$. Determine the constant rate of change and the starting value, write an equation in slope-intercept form, and give the output when $x=9$.

Answer / solution

The rate is $\frac{23-11}{6-2}=3$. Using $(2,11)$ gives $11=3(2)+b$, so $b=5$. The equation is $y=3x+5$, and when $x=9$, $y=32$.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: Recognize constant rate of change and represent linear relationships with tables, graphs, equations, slope, and starting value.

Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit PROC-MULTISTEP-CHAIN-01

I can use key features of a line to interpret a relationship.

context_to_graph

Example 4

U2-S22-NOTESEXAMPLE-04 · Notes Example · family context_to_graph · Context -> Graph · DOK 2

A gym charges a 30-dollar joining fee plus 8 dollars per visit. Sketch a graph with visits on the horizontal axis and total cost on the vertical axis. Identify what the intercept means.

Answer / solution

The graph is a line with slope $8$ and vertical intercept $30$. The intercept represents the starting total cost.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can use key features of a line to interpret a relationship.
Representation: Context -> Graph
Evidence/job: Translate a meaningful linear context into graph features.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit REPTRANS-GRAPH-TABLE-EQUATION-01

scale_error

Example 6

U2-S22-NOTESEXAMPLE-06 · Notes Example · family scale_error · Graph / Equation · DOK 2-3

On a graph, each horizontal grid step is 2 units and each vertical grid step is 5 units. A student moves right 1 square and up 1 square and calls the slope 1. Identify the error and correct it.

Answer / solution

The slope is $5/2$, not 1, because grid squares represent different numerical changes.

wtc_graph_features

WTC

U2-S22-WTC-01 · WTC · family wtc_graph_features · Graph + Context · DOK 2

The graph shows a savings account growing at a steady pace. Do not write an equation yet.

(a) What amount is present at week 0? (b) Describe how the savings change from one week to the next. (c) Name two points that would help someone recreate the graph accurately. (d) Predict a reasonable value at week 7.

Answer / solution

The account starts at 40 dollars and increases by 20 dollars per week. Useful points include $(0,40)$ and $(1,60)$. At week 7, the graph gives 180 dollars.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can use key features of a line to interpret a relationship.
Representation: Graph + Context
Evidence/job: Preview intercept, constant change, and graph reconstruction from one situation.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit WTC-FRQ-PREVIEW-01

I can build graphs of linear functions using slope and intercept information.

Create a coordinate graph of $y=-2x+4$. Label the intercept and one additional point that demonstrates the slope. Then state what each coordinate of that second point means mathematically.

Answer / solution

A correct graph crosses at $(0,4)$ and has slope $-2$. One possible second point is $(2,0)$.

mg01_graph_check

V5 Question 5

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-37 · Secure Summative · family mg01_graph_check · Equation / Graph Reasoning · DOK 2-3

A student intends to graph a line with $y$-intercept $-2$ and slope $3/2$. They plot $(0,-2)$ and $(2,1)$. Verify whether the second point is correct and describe the graph that should result.

Answer / solution

Yes. From $(0,-2)$, moving right 2 and up 3 reaches $(2,1)$. The line has slope $3/2$ and crosses the $y$-axis at $-2$.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: Interpret and create coordinate-plane graphs, including ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts, in mathematical and contextual situations.

V6 Question 7

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-47 · Secure Summative · family mg01_graph_check · Graph + Equation Check · DOK 2-3

A student says the graph represents $y=\frac{3}{2}x-2$ and that $(4,4)$ should lie on the line. Verify both claims using the graph's intercept, scale, and rate of change.

Answer / solution

Both claims are correct. The graph crosses the $y$-axis at $-2$ and rises 3 units for every 2 units to the right, so its equation is $y=\frac{3}{2}x-2$. Substituting $x=4$ gives $y=4$, so $(4,4)$ lies on the line.

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-33 · Secure Summative · family mg01_route · Graph / Ordered Pairs · DOK 2-3

A map uses a coordinate grid. A checkpoint is at $(-3,4)$ and the trail crosses the vertical axis at $(0,1)$. Plot both points, draw the line through them, and explain how the ordered pairs determine the direction of the line.

Answer / solution

The line through $(-3,4)$ and $(0,1)$ has slope $-1$ and $y$-intercept 1. The coordinates determine horizontal and vertical positions, and moving right 3 corresponds to moving down 3.

A cooling sensor records the values below. Create a coordinate-plane graph with time on the horizontal axis and temperature on the vertical axis. Choose and label an appropriate scale, plot the three ordered pairs, and identify the vertical-axis intercept and its meaning.

Time (min)Temperature ($^\circ$C)
$0$$18$
$2$$14$
$4$$10$
Answer / solution

A correct graph includes $(0,18)$, $(2,14)$, and $(4,10)$ with labeled axes and a consistent scale. The vertical-axis intercept is $(0,18)$, meaning the temperature is $18^\circ$C at time 0.

I can apply slope-intercept structure to connect rate and starting value.

equation_from_context

Example 4

U2-S23-NOTESEXAMPLE-04 · Notes Example · family equation_from_context · Context -> Equation · DOK 2

A bike rental costs 12 dollars to start plus 4 dollars per hour. Let $h$ be hours and $C$ be total cost. Write $C$ as a function of $h$. Identify the hourly charge and the initial charge in your equation.

Answer / solution

$C=4h+12$. The slope $4$ is the change in C per unit of h; the intercept $12$ is the starting value.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can apply slope-intercept structure to connect rate and starting value.
Representation: Context -> Equation
Evidence/job: Connect contextual rate and starting value to slope-intercept structure.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit REPTRANS-TRANSLATE-VERBAL-SYMBOLIC-01

YTI 4

U2-S23-NOTESYTI-04 · Notes YTI · family equation_from_context · Context -> Equation · DOK 2

A streaming service charges 9 dollars to start plus 6 dollars per month. Let $m$ be months and $C$ be total cost. Write $C$ as a function of $m$. Identify the monthly charge and the initial charge in your equation.

Answer / solution

$C=6m+9$. The slope $6$ is the monthly change and the intercept $9$ is the starting charge.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can apply slope-intercept structure to connect rate and starting value.
Representation: Context -> Equation
Evidence/job: Connect contextual rate and starting value to slope-intercept structure.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit REPTRANS-TRANSLATE-VERBAL-SYMBOLIC-01

Question 10

U2-S23-PRACTICESET1-10 · Practice Set 1 · family equation_from_context · Context -> Equation · DOK 2

A student has 25 dollars and saves 7 dollars each week. Let $w$ be weeks and $S$ be savings. Write $S$ as a function of $w$. Identify the weekly change and the starting savings.

Answer / solution

$S=7w+25$. The slope $7$ is the change in S per unit of w; the intercept $25$ is the starting value.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can apply slope-intercept structure to connect rate and starting value.
Representation: Context -> Equation
Evidence/job: Connect contextual rate and starting value to slope-intercept structure.

A tank starts with 27 liters and drains 5 liters each minute. Let $t$ be minutes and $V$ be volume. Write $V$ as a function of $t$. Identify the change and starting volume.

Answer / solution

$V=-5t+27$. The slope $-5$ is the change per minute and the intercept $27$ is the starting volume.

interpret_m_b

Example 5

U2-S23-NOTESEXAMPLE-05 · Notes Example · family interpret_m_b · Equation · DOK 2

For $y=3x+8$, explain the roles of $m$ and $b$. Then give one ordered pair that must lie on the line.

Answer / solution

$m=3$ is the constant rate of change and $b=8$ is the value when $x=0$. For example, $(2,14)$ lies on the line.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can apply slope-intercept structure to connect rate and starting value.
Representation: Equation
Evidence/job: Interpret slope-intercept parameters as structural features.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01

wtc_rule_from_table

WTC

U2-S23-WTC-01 · WTC · family wtc_rule_from_table · Table + Context · DOK 2

A local club tracks the total amount collected after selling tickets. The table records a steady pattern.

Tickets soldTotal collected (dollars)
018
123
228
333

(a) What appears to be the starting amount? (b) How much does the total change for each additional ticket? (c) Write a rule in words that could reproduce the table. (d) Without using formal slope-intercept vocabulary, predict the total for 6 tickets.

Answer / solution

The starting amount is 18 dollars and each additional ticket adds 5 dollars. A rule is “start at 18 and add 5 for each ticket.” For 6 tickets, the total is 48 dollars.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can apply slope-intercept structure to connect rate and starting value.
Representation: Table + Context
Evidence/job: Preview rate/start structure before formal equation writing.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit WTC-FRQ-PREVIEW-01

I can formulate linear equations from graphs, tables, and situations.

equation_from_graph

Example 3

U2-S23-NOTESEXAMPLE-03 · Notes Example · family equation_from_graph · Graph -> Equation · DOK 2

Write an equation in slope-intercept form for the graphed line. Use graph features as evidence.

Answer / solution

The graph has slope $-2$ and $y$-intercept $5$, so $y=-2x+5$.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can formulate linear equations from graphs, tables, and situations.
Representation: Graph -> Equation
Evidence/job: Translate graph features into an equation.

Example 2

Write an equation in slope-intercept form for the relationship in the table.

$x$$y$
$-2$$-4$
$0$$-3$
$2$$-2$
$4$$-1$
Answer / solution

The constant rate is $\frac{1}{2}$ and the value at $x=0$ is $-3$, so $y=\frac{1}{2}x-3$.

I can design equivalent equations that represent the same linear relationship.

equivalent_reverse

Example 6

U2-S23-NOTESEXAMPLE-06 · Notes Example · family equivalent_reverse · Equation · DOK 2-3

A linear relationship is represented by $y=3x-2$. Write a different equation that is equivalent to it after moving all terms to one side, and verify that $(7,19)$ satisfies both forms.

Answer / solution

One equivalent form is $3x-y-2=0$. Substituting $x=7$ and $y=19$ makes both equations true.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can design equivalent equations that represent the same linear relationship.
Representation: Equation
Evidence/job: Create and verify an equivalent equation for the same line.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit COMPARE-COMPETING-METHODS-01

YTI 6

U2-S23-NOTESYTI-06 · Notes YTI · family equivalent_reverse · Equation · DOK 2-3

A linear relationship is represented by $y=-2x+7$. Write a different equation that is equivalent to it after moving all terms to one side, and verify that $(3,1)$ satisfies both forms.

Answer / solution

One equivalent form is $-2x-y+7=0$. Substituting $x=3$ and $y=1$ makes both equations true.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can design equivalent equations that represent the same linear relationship.
Representation: Equation
Evidence/job: Create and verify an equivalent equation for the same line.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit COMPARE-COMPETING-METHODS-01

Question 17

U2-S23-PRACTICESET1-17 · Practice Set 1 · family equivalent_reverse · Equation · DOK 2-3

A linear relationship is represented by $y=-2x+3$. Write a different equation that is equivalent to it after moving all terms to one side, and verify that $(6,-9)$ satisfies both forms.

Answer / solution

One equivalent form is $-2x-y+3=0$. Substituting $x=6$ and $y=-9$ makes both equations true.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can design equivalent equations that represent the same linear relationship.
Representation: Equation
Evidence/job: Create and verify an equivalent equation for the same line.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit COMPARE-COMPETING-METHODS-01

I can use slope and intercept information to interpret similarities and differences.

context_compare

Example 4

U2-S24-NOTESEXAMPLE-04 · Notes Example · family context_compare · Context · DOK 2

Account A starts at 50 dollars and gains 8 dollars per week. Account B starts at 80 dollars and gains 5 dollars per week. Compare the rates and starting values. Which feature answers “changes faster,” and which answers “starts larger”?

Answer / solution

A grows faster (8 vs 5 dollars/week); B starts higher (80 vs 50 dollars).

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can use slope and intercept information to interpret similarities and differences.
Representation: Context
Evidence/job: Compare two contextual linear models by invariant features.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit MODEL-COMPARE-TWO-LINEAR-01

crossover_compare

Example 6

U2-S24-NOTESEXAMPLE-06 · Notes Example · family crossover_compare · Equation + Comparison · DOK 2-3

For Relationship A, $y=3x+8$, and Relationship B, $y=2x+14$, find the input where their outputs are equal and identify which relationship has the greater rate of change.

Answer / solution

Set the equations equal: the outputs match at $x=6$, where $y=26$. A has the greater rate because its slope is 3.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can use slope and intercept information to interpret similarities and differences.
Representation: Equation + Comparison
Evidence/job: Compare linear models and locate an equal-output point.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit MODEL-COMPARE-TWO-LINEAR-01

U2-S24-CYU-06 · CYU · family crossover_compare · Equation + Comparison · DOK 2-3

For Relationship A, $y=2x+3$, and Relationship B, $y=3x-1$, find the input where their outputs are equal and identify which relationship has the greater rate of change.

Answer / solution

Set the equations equal: the outputs match at $x=4$, where $y=11$. B has the greater rate because its slope is $3$.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can use slope and intercept information to interpret similarities and differences.
Representation: Equation + Comparison
Evidence/job: Compare linear models and locate an equal-output point.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit MODEL-COMPARE-TWO-LINEAR-01

graph_vs_equation

Example 2

U2-S24-NOTESEXAMPLE-02 · Notes Example · family graph_vs_equation · Graph + Equation · DOK 2

Relationships A and B are the two graphed lines. Relationship C is $y=-x+3$. Order A, B, and C from greatest to least rate of change, and compare their starting values.

Answer / solution

Relationship B has rate $2$ and starting value $-2$; Relationship A has rate $1$ and starting value $2$; Relationship C has rate $-1$ and starting value $3$. The rates order $B\gt A\gt C$. The starting values order $C\gt A\gt B$.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can use slope and intercept information to interpret similarities and differences.
Representation: Graph + Equation
Evidence/job: Compare slope and intercept across graph/equation forms.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit REPTRANS-GRAPH-TABLE-EQUATION-01

wtc_compare_plans

WTC

U2-S24-WTC-01 · WTC · family wtc_compare_plans · Graph + Context · DOK 2-3

Two plans are graphed on the same axes. Focus on what the lines reveal, not on which one merely looks higher at one point.

(a) Which plan starts with the greater cost? (b) Which plan changes faster per hour? (c) Around what time do the costs become equal? (d) Explain why both starting value and rate matter when comparing the plans.

Answer / solution

Plan B starts higher (14 vs 8 dollars), while Plan A changes faster (3 vs 2 dollars per hour). They are equal at about 6 hours. A higher start does not imply faster growth, so both features matter.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can use slope and intercept information to interpret similarities and differences.
Representation: Graph + Context
Evidence/job: Preview comparison by rate and starting value using one shared graph.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit WTC-FRQ-PREVIEW-01

I can construct comparison models for linear relationships shown in different forms.

I can critique conclusions about linear relationships using evidence from representations.

claim_critique

Example 5

U2-S24-NOTESEXAMPLE-05 · Notes Example · family claim_critique · Graph / Context · DOK 3

Two lines have the same slope but different $y$-intercepts. Casey says they represent the same relationship. Decide whether equal slopes are enough to make two linear relationships identical.

Answer / solution

False. Equal slope means parallel change, but different intercepts give different output values.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can critique conclusions about linear relationships using evidence from representations.
Representation: Graph / Context
Evidence/job: Critique a comparison claim using slope/intercept evidence.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit ERROR-CRITIQUE-CLAIM-01

YTI 5

U2-S24-NOTESYTI-05 · Notes YTI · family claim_critique · Graph / Context · DOK 3

At $x=2$, Relationship A has the larger output, so Lee says A must always grow faster. Explain why one output value cannot establish which relationship grows faster.

Answer / solution

A single output does not determine growth rate. Compare slopes/rates separately from starting values.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can critique conclusions about linear relationships using evidence from representations.
Representation: Graph / Context
Evidence/job: Critique a comparison claim using slope/intercept evidence.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit ERROR-CRITIQUE-CLAIM-01

Question 13

U2-S24-PRACTICESET1-13 · Practice Set 1 · family claim_critique · Graph / Context · DOK 3

At input $x=2$, Relationship A has output 29 and Relationship B has output 30. A student says the relationship with the larger output at this one input must always have the greater rate of change. Critique the claim and state what evidence is needed.

Answer / solution

The claim is not justified. One output value mixes the effects of rate and starting value; compare changes over equal input intervals or determine each slope before deciding which relationship grows faster.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can critique conclusions about linear relationships using evidence from representations.
Representation: Graph / Context
Evidence/job: Critique a comparison claim using slope/intercept evidence.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit ERROR-CRITIQUE-CLAIM-01

Question 14

A graph of Line A uses 1 unit per grid square; a graph of Line B uses 10 units per grid square. Line B looks steeper, so Pat says B must have the greater slope. Explain why visual steepness alone is not enough when the graph scales differ.

Answer / solution

The conclusion is not justified from visual steepness alone because the axis scales differ. Compare numerical rate values.

The rates of change are also needed. A relationship that starts lower can overtake another if its rate is greater.

A student compares two tables using only the last displayed output and ignores the input intervals. Explain the flaw.

Answer / solution

The rate depends on change in output relative to change in input, so input intervals and starting values must be considered.

Question 1

U2-S24-EXITTICKETB-01 · Exit Ticket B · family review_evidence · Equation · DOK 1-2

A relationship is represented by $y=x^2+2$. Identify its function family and give one feature that supports your choice.

Answer / solution

The relationship is quadratic; the equation contains an $x^2$ term and does not have a constant first difference.

Answer / solution

mg04_critique

V1 Question 8

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-08 · Secure Summative · family mg04_critique · Graph / Scale · DOK 2-3

Two linear graphs are drawn in different windows. Graph A looks steeper, but its vertical axis counts by 20 while Graph B counts by 2. A student claims A must have the greater slope. Critique the claim and state what numerical evidence would settle the comparison.

Answer / solution

The claim is not justified by visual steepness because the scales differ. Compute each rate as numerical change in output divided by numerical change in input, or translate both to equations/tables, then compare slopes.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: Compare relationships across equations, graphs, tables, visual patterns, and contexts and justify conclusions with representation evidence.
Representation: Graph / Scale

mg04_data_claim

V5 Question 8

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-40 · Secure Summative · family mg04_data_claim · Equation / Comparison · DOK 2-3

Relationship A has rate 1.5 and starting value 12. Relationship B has rate 2.5 and starting value 4. A student says B is always larger because its rate is larger. Decide whether the claim is true, and support your conclusion by finding when the outputs are equal.

Answer / solution

The claim is false. Solve $1.5x+12=2.5x+4$ to get $x=8$. Before $x=8$, A is larger; after $x=8$, B is larger.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: Compare relationships across equations, graphs, tables, visual patterns, and contexts and justify conclusions with representation evidence.
Representation: Equation / Comparison
Evidence/job: Secure independent alternate-form evidence for U2-MG04.
Mastery Goal: U2-MG04

V6 Question 5

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-45 · Secure Summative · family mg04_data_claim · Scatter Plot + Equations · DOK 2-3

Use the scatter plot to compare two proposed linear models for the data: Model A is $y=3x+4$ and Model B is $y=4x+2$. A student claims Model B is better because it has the greater rate of change. Decide whether the claim is supported by the data. Compare the model predictions with the plotted values at $x=4$ and $x=6$ as part of your evidence.

Answer / solution

The claim is not supported. At $x=4$, the plotted output is about 16; Model A predicts 16 and Model B predicts 18. At $x=6$, the plotted output is about 22.5; Model A predicts 22 and Model B predicts 26. Model A is better supported by these data.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: Compare relationships across equations, graphs, tables, visual patterns, and contexts and justify conclusions with representation evidence.
Representation: Scatter Plot + Equations
Evidence/job: Secure independent alternate-form evidence for U2-MG04.
Mastery Goal: U2-MG04
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit ERROR-CRITIQUE-CLAIM-01

mg04_decision

V5 Question 4

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-36 · Secure Summative · family mg04_decision · Context + Equation · DOK 2-3

Option A costs 18 dollars plus 3 dollars per visit. Option B is represented by $C=5v+6$. For 4 visits and for 10 visits, determine which option is less expensive. Explain how rate and starting cost create the change in which option is better.

Answer / solution

At 4 visits: A=30, B=26, so B is cheaper. At 10 visits: A=48, B=56, so A is cheaper. B starts lower but increases faster.

V6 Question 1

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-41 · Secure Summative · family mg04_graph_compare · Equations -> Shared Graph + Comparison · DOK 2-3

Graph Relationship A, $y=-x+8$, and Relationship B, $y=2x-1$, on the same coordinate plane. Label the point where the lines intersect. Use the graph and the equations to compare starting values and rates of change, then state which relationship has the greater output for $x\gt 3$.

Answer / solution

The lines intersect at $(3,5)$. Relationship A has starting value 8 and rate $-1$; Relationship B has starting value $-1$ and rate 2. For $x\gt 3$, Relationship B has the greater output.

wtc_scatter_model

WTC

U2-S25-WTC-01 · WTC · family wtc_scatter_model · Scatter Plot + Context · DOK 2-3

A student measures a plant once each week. The data do not fall on a perfect line.

(a) Describe the overall trend. (b) Sketch mentally where a reasonable straight trend line would run through the cloud of points. (c) Estimate the height at week 5 from the data pattern. (d) Explain why a useful model does not require every point to lie exactly on one line.

Answer / solution

The data show a strong positive, roughly linear trend. A reasonable line would pass through the middle of the points. Around week 5 the height is about 14–15 cm. Real measurements vary, so a model summarizes the trend rather than matching every observation exactly.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can design linear models that represent patterns in real-world data.
Representation: Scatter Plot + Context
Evidence/job: Preview linear modeling as trend description rather than perfect fit.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit WTC-FRQ-PREVIEW-01

I can predict values using a linear model and interpret the result.

interpolation_extrapolation

Question 13

U2-S25-PRACTICESET2-13 · Practice Set 2 · family interpolation_extrapolation · Context / Data Range · DOK 2

Measurements were collected for $x=2$ through $x=10$. Predicting at $x=7$: classify the prediction as interpolation or extrapolation and explain which is generally safer when the observed trend is only approximately linear.

Answer / solution

This is interpolation because the requested input is inside the observed range. Interpolation is generally safer because it stays within the range where the pattern was observed.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can predict values using a linear model and interpret the result.

A model for a subscription total is $C=-12m+194$, where $m$ is months and $C$ is dollars. Interpret both the slope and intercept in context.

Answer / solution

The slope $-12$ represents the change in cost per month; the intercept $194$ represents the starting cost.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can predict values using a linear model and interpret the result.
Representation: Context + Equation
Evidence/job: Interpret model parameters in context.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01

use_model_prediction

Example 2

U2-S25-NOTESEXAMPLE-02 · Notes Example · family use_model_prediction · Equation / Context · DOK 2

A linear model is $y=-4x+50$. Use the model to predict $y$ when $x=7$ and interpret the result as a model prediction rather than an exact observed value.

Answer / solution

The model predicts $y=22$ when $x=7$. This is the value produced by the fitted relationship; actual data may differ.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can predict values using a linear model and interpret the result.

I can assess whether a linear model fits a data situation well enough for the question being asked.

fit_reasonableness

Example 4

U2-S25-NOTESEXAMPLE-04 · Notes Example · family fit_reasonableness · Context / Data · DOK 3

A scatter plot curves upward more and more as $x$ increases. A student says a straight line fits perfectly. Assess the reasoning.

Answer / solution

The claim is unreasonable because the changing steepness shows a nonconstant rate; a single line will miss the systematic curve.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can assess whether a linear model fits a data situation well enough for the question being asked.
Representation: Context / Data
Evidence/job: Assess model fit or prediction limits for the question being asked.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit VERIFY-REASONABLENESS-01

YTI 4

U2-S25-NOTESYTI-04 · Notes YTI · family fit_reasonableness · Context / Data · DOK 3

Data were collected for ages 10 through 16. A student uses the linear model to predict a value at age 90. Assess the reasoning.

Answer / solution

The prediction is a very distant extrapolation and may be unreliable because it is far outside the observed range.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can assess whether a linear model fits a data situation well enough for the question being asked.
Representation: Context / Data
Evidence/job: Assess model fit or prediction limits for the question being asked.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit VERIFY-REASONABLENESS-01

Question 10

U2-S25-PRACTICESET1-10 · Practice Set 1 · family fit_reasonableness · Context / Data · DOK 3

A data set is shown below. A student says a straight line fits the pattern perfectly. Assess the reasoning using the changes in the outputs.

$x$$y$
$0$$15$
$1$$18$
$2$$24$
$3$$33$
Answer / solution

The claim is unreasonable because equal input steps produce changing output differences (3, 6, then 9), so the data do not have a constant rate of change and cannot lie perfectly on one line.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can assess whether a linear model fits a data situation well enough for the question being asked.
Representation: Context / Data
Evidence/job: Assess model fit or prediction limits for the question being asked.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit VERIFY-REASONABLENESS-01

Question 12

U2-S25-PRACTICESET1-12 · Practice Set 1 · family fit_reasonableness · Context / Data · DOK 3

Observed values are 20, 25, 30, 35 while a model predicts 19, 26, 29, 36. A student rejects the model because no prediction is exact. Assess the reasoning.

Answer / solution

That is not a sound reason. A model can be useful when small errors are acceptable for the question; exact matches are not required.

interpolation_extrapolation

Example 5

U2-S25-NOTESEXAMPLE-05 · Notes Example · family interpolation_extrapolation · Context / Data Range · DOK 2

Measurements were collected for $x=2$ through $x=10$. Predicting at $x=14$: classify the prediction as interpolation or extrapolation and explain which is generally safer when the observed trend is only approximately linear.

Answer / solution

This is extrapolation because the requested input is outside the observed range. Interpolation is generally safer because it stays within the range where the pattern was observed.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can assess whether a linear model fits a data situation well enough for the question being asked.
Representation: Context / Data Range
Evidence/job: Distinguish interpolation from extrapolation and discuss reliability.
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit PREDICT-EXTRAPOLATE-MODEL-01

CYU 5

U2-S25-CYU-05 · CYU · family interpolation_extrapolation · Context / Data Range · DOK 2

Data were observed for inputs from $x=3$ through $x=9$. A model is used to predict the output at $x=5$. Classify the prediction as interpolation or extrapolation and explain which is generally safer when the observed trend is only approximately linear.

Answer / solution

This is interpolation because the requested input is inside the observed range. Interpolation is generally safer because it stays within the range where the pattern was observed.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: I can assess whether a linear model fits a data situation well enough for the question being asked.
Representation: Context / Data Range
Evidence/job: Distinguish interpolation from extrapolation and discuss reliability.

Answer / solution

$C=9h+69$.

mastery_evidence

Question 1

U2-S25-EXITTICKETC-01 · Exit Ticket C · family mastery_evidence · Equation / Ordered Pair · DOK 2

A pattern follows the rule $y=-x+8$. Find the output for $x=3$ and give the ordered pair.

Answer / solution

The output is $5$, so the ordered pair is $(3,5)$.

A pattern begins with 5 at input 0 and changes by 4 for each increase of 1 in the input. Build a four-row input-output table for inputs 0 through 3, write a rule, and give the ordered pair for input 6.

Answer / solution
$x$$y$
05
19
213
317

Rule: $y=4x+5$. The ordered pair at input 6 is $(6,29)$.

mg02_reverse

V5 Question 7

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-39 · Secure Summative · family mg02_reverse · Ordered Pairs + Equation · DOK 2-3

A linear input-output rule gives the ordered pairs $(0,9)$, $(2,15)$, and $(5,24)$. Determine the rule, then find the input that produces output 30.

Answer / solution

The rate is 3 and the starting value is 9, so $y=3x+9$. Solving $30=3x+9$ gives $x=7$.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: Represent patterns with input-output tables, verbal rules, symbolic rules, and ordered pairs, then use the rule to extend the relationship.
Representation: Ordered Pairs + Equation
Evidence/job: Secure independent alternate-form evidence for U2-MG02.
Mastery Goal: U2-MG02

V6 Question 8

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-48 · Secure Summative · family mg02_rule_check · Rule + Table / Error Analysis · DOK 2-3

The rule $y=2x-4$ is supposed to generate the table. One row is wrong. Identify and correct that row, then use the rule to find the input that produces output 14.

$x$$y$
$-1$$-6$
$0$$-4$
$3$$3$
$5$$6$
Answer / solution

The row $(3,3)$ is wrong because $2(3)-4=2$, so it should be $(3,2)$. Solving $14=2x-4$ gives $x=9$.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: Represent patterns with input-output tables, verbal rules, symbolic rules, and ordered pairs, then use the rule to extend the relationship.
Representation: Rule + Table / Error Analysis
Evidence/job: Secure independent alternate-form evidence for U2-MG02.
Mastery Goal: U2-MG02
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit ERROR-CRITIQUE-CLAIM-01

mg02_verbal

V5 Question 2

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-34 · Secure Summative · family mg02_verbal · Context -> Rule + Pairs · DOK 2-3

A machine produces 7 parts before a timed run begins and then adds 4 parts each minute. Write a verbal rule, a symbolic rule, and three ordered pairs for minutes 0, 2, and 5.

Answer / solution

Verbal rule: start at 7 and add 4 per minute. Symbolic rule: $P=4m+7$. Ordered pairs: $(0,7)$, $(2,15)$, $(5,27)$.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: Represent patterns with input-output tables, verbal rules, symbolic rules, and ordered pairs, then use the rule to extend the relationship.

V6 Question 6

U2-SSUM-SECURESUMMATIV-46 · Secure Summative · family mg02_pattern_infer · Pattern Data -> Ordered Pairs + Rule · DOK 2-3

A tile pattern has 6 tiles at Stage 0, 10 tiles at Stage 1, and 14 tiles at Stage 2. Represent the pattern with three ordered pairs, write a symbolic rule for the number of tiles $T$ in terms of stage $s$, use the rule to predict Stage 7, and state the rule in words.

Answer / solution

The ordered pairs are $(0,6)$, $(1,10)$, and $(2,14)$. The rule is $T=4s+6$. At Stage 7, $T=34$. In words: start with 6 tiles at Stage 0 and add 4 tiles for each new stage.

Alignment / evidenceExact target: Represent patterns with input-output tables, verbal rules, symbolic rules, and ordered pairs, then use the rule to extend the relationship.
Representation: Pattern Data -> Ordered Pairs + Rule
Evidence/job: Secure independent alternate-form evidence for U2-MG02.
Mastery Goal: U2-MG02
Source basis: Current Unit Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework section enrichment + Toolkit REPTRANS-MULTIPLE-SIMULTANEOUS-01
